## SECTION 1 — VISUAL FOUNDATIONS (GLOBAL SETTINGS)  
  
## 1. SKIN TONE UNITY  
Always maintain a **humanly diverse, globally accurate range** of skin tones without flattening identity.  
* Color science: rich melanin capture, natural undertones   
* Avoid digital plasticity — retain pores, micro shadows, real texture   
* Use **Kodak Portra 400** film emulation for warmth + natural highlight roll-off   
* Maintain consistent tone mapping so all characters exist in the same world   
**Rendering Command:** “Realistic multi-ethnic casting with accurate melanin representation, preserved pore detail, preserved skin moisture specularity, no over-smoothing, Portra 400 highlight roll-off.”  
  
## 2. MICROGRAPHICS + MICROTEXT  
RANAAN branding must appear **physically integrated**, not AI-stamped.  
* Embroidery: visible thread fibers, directional stitch pattern   
* Silk-screen: slight ink bleed, micro cracking on edges   
* Woven labels: frayed threads, stitch tension   
* Hats: curved brim texture, shadow stitching   
**Rendering Command:** “Authentic textile behavior: stitch-level embroidery, micro-fray, woven label edge fibers, screen print ink texture, logo integrates physically with garment.”  
  
## 3. CAMERA SYSTEM  
All shots use **1990s photography physics**, not modern clarity.  
* Film cameras: Ricoh GR1, Contax T2, Nikon F3   
* Lenses: 28mm, 35mm, 45mm (street / editorial)   
* Aperture: f/2.8–f/4 (shallow but controlled DOF)   
* Grain: 600–1600 ISO pushed film   
* Rare fisheye for skate motion   
**9:16 output:** --ar 9:16  
  
## 4. LIGHTING ARCHITECTURE  
90s skate lighting = **raw, available, imperfect**  
* Overcast Parisian soft light   
* Harsh LA sun + lens flare   
* Fluorescent Berlin green tint   
* Neon Tokyo wet-reflection glow   
* Flash-burn Polaroid   
* Natural bounce from stone, metal, street signage   
**Rendering Command:** “Authentic 90s lighting imperfections: halation, bloom, reflective neon pools, soft overcast diffusion, fluorescent green shift.”  
  
## 5. COLOR PALETTE (CAMPAIGN-WIDE)  
The RANAAN palette blends street grit + editorial minimalism:  
* Asphalt Blue   
* Dusty Concrete   
* Neon Magenta (Tokyo)   
* Acid Green (Berlin)   
* Faded Denim Blue   
* Desert Amber (LA)   
* Warm Portra Skin Tones   
**Profile:** “Kodachrome 64 saturation + Portra 400 skin + Fuji 90’s street color shifts.”  
  
## 6. TEXTURE & MATERIAL BEHAVIOR  
* Denim: sanded edges, whiskering, matte fiber   
* Nylon: crinkle, specular peaks   
* Concrete: porous, chipped, dirty   
* Metal rails: scratch clusters, wear patterns   
* Skate deck: concave shape, chipped tail, grip tape grain   
**Rendering Command:** “Real material physics: directional roughness, micro-scratches, dust, grime accumulation, grip tape granularity.”  
  
## 7. COMPOSITIONAL DNA  
90s skate magazines used **anti-perfect** framing:  
* Dutch angles   
* Fisheye distortion   
* Center-heavy portraits   
* Negative space used violently   
* Imperfect cropping   
* Subjects often off-balance or mid-motion   
**Rendering Command:** “Documentary composition, imperfect framing, dynamic asymmetry, compression from street geometry.”  
  
## 8. CULTURAL AUTHENTICITY  
Respect and accurately portray each city:  
* **Tokyo:** signage, vending machines, wet reflections   
* **LA:** hills, palm shadows, sun flare, stucco houses   
* **SoHo NYC:** cast iron buildings, cobblestones   
* **Paris:** Haussmann lines, Métro typography   
* **Berlin:** concrete slabs, fluorescent stations   
No stereotypes. Only lived-in visual truth.  
  
## 9. EDITORIAL ELEMENTS  
* Dust, film scratches   
* Timestamp in corner   
* Polaroid borders   
* Skate mag text overlays (minimal)   
Typography references:  
* 90s Transworld   
* Thrasher condensed heavy   
* Helvetica Neue Condensed for clean captions   
  
## 10. FILM EMULATION & COLOR SCIENCE  
* Grain must be **additive**, not fake   
* Color halation on highlights   
* Slight chromatic fringing   
* VHS streaks for motion pieces   
* Kodak warmth + Fuji greens   
**Rendering Command:** “True 90s analog texture stack: grain, halation, chromatic fringe, VHS interlacing for motion frames.”

from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter  
from reportlab.lib import colors  
  
doc = SimpleDocTemplate("/mnt/data/RANAAN_Brand_Deck.pdf", pagesize=letter)  
styles = getSampleStyleSheet()  
story = []  
  
content = """  
RANAAN Global Skate Campaign — Brand Deck  
  
1. Visual Identity  
- 90s skate editorial tone  
- Global city authenticity: Tokyo, LA, NYC, Paris, Berlin  
- Unified color science (Kodachrome + Portra)  
  
2. Color Palette  
- Asphalt Blue  
- Dusty Concrete  
- Neon Magenta  
- Acid Green  
- Faded Denim  
- Desert Amber  
  
3. Typography  
- Helvetica Neue Condensed  
- Thrasher-style bold headers  
- Minimal microtext branding  
  
4. Film Emulation  
- Grain: ISO 600–1600  
- Halation, dust, fringing  
- Polaroid and VHS overlays  
  
5. Brand Application  
- Embroidery with stitch detail  
- Woven labels  
- Silk-screen micrographics  
  
6. Composition DNA  
- Dutch angles  
- Fisheye for motion  
- Imperfect framing  
- 9:16 portrait as master format  
"""  
  
for line in content.split("\n"):  
    story.append(Paragraph(line, styles["Normal"]))  
    story.append(Spacer(1, 12))  
  
doc.build(story)

# 🧩 SECTION 3 — UNIVERSAL HYBRID ENGINE (FULL MASTER PROMPT)  
**This is the “super prompt.”** Everything else (MJ versions, A/B tests, motion notes) derives from this.  
  
# 🌐 MASTER 5-LAYER MOTION-READY PROMPT ENGINE  
*(With Dual Switch + Hybrid Override System)*  
  
<STYLE_MODE> = [1–15 or name]  
  
Create a hybrid-driven visual generated using the selected style mode.   
Use the following 5-layer architecture with strict structure:  
  
---------------------------------------  
LAYER 1 — SUBJECT & STATE  
---------------------------------------  
Primary subject: <INSERT SUBJECT OR PRODUCT>    
Action / pose / state: <INSERT ACTION>    
Emotional tone: <INSERT EMOTION>    
Physical descriptors: <INSERT DETAILS>    
If style cross-pollination is beneficial, subtly integrate compatible traits   
from adjacent styles (see Style DNA).  
  
---------------------------------------  
LAYER 2 — ENVIRONMENT (STYLE-SWITCHED)  
---------------------------------------  
Set the environment based on STYLE_MODE logic:  
  
1 punk_flatlay → overhead table, matte paper    
2 maison_rapito_collage → luxury collage on black    
3 stussy_skate_ad → outdoor skate/urban industrial    
4 burning_tire_editorial → harsh sun + fire background    
5 stussy_pdf_catalog → MacOS PDF layout    
6 not_yet_home_caps → tan seamless backdrop    
7 lowrfs_trillizos_ad → color-block portrait grids    
8 032c_priority_mail → USPS form base + graffiti    
9 xp_global_catalog → XP wallpaper + product rows    
10 idk_ask_chatgpt_cap → MacOS windows + dialogs    
11 windows95_nostalgia → Win95 interface desktop    
12 crt_outfit_selector → CRT game UI on TV    
14 nyc_subway_leatherjacket → NYC subway platform    
15 stussy_cape_town_magazine → skatepark + Table Mountain    
  
Apply hybrid logic:    
If STYLE_MODE allows cross-pollination, introduce subtle UI elements, sticker motifs,   
CRT scanlines, graffiti marks, browser chrome, or zine scraps where compositionally useful.  
  
---------------------------------------  
LAYER 3 — LIGHTING (STYLE-LOCKED WITH HYBRID SOFTENING)  
---------------------------------------  
Lighting defaults by style, but can borrow:  
  
• harsh sun / golden hour (#3, #4, #15)    
• soft studio (#6, #7)    
• interface glow (#9, #10, #11)    
• CRT emissions (#12)    
• overhead flat-lay (#1, #2)  
  
Maintain realistic shadow behavior based on the chosen era.  
  
---------------------------------------  
LAYER 4 — CAMERA (PER STYLE, WITH VARIABLE OVERRIDES)  
---------------------------------------  
Select camera logic:  
  
• Overhead grid (1)    
• Collage perspective (2)    
• Low-angle skate hero (3)    
• Documentary low angle (4)    
• Editorial catalog framing (5, 6)    
• Portrait panel close-ups (7)    
• Form/document top-down (8)    
• Desktop UI head-on (9, 10, 11)    
• CRT straight-on slight tilt (12)    
• Street candid flash (14)    
• Landscape elevated (15)  
  
Set lens + aperture:  
24–35mm (skate, editorial)    
50–85mm (portraits)    
Orthographic (UI, flatlays)  
  
---------------------------------------  
LAYER 5 — PRODUCTION VALUE + OVERLAYS  
---------------------------------------  
Apply hybrid overlay grammar:  
  
• microtexts: timestamps, city lists, product codes, UI labels    
• micrographics: stickers, stars, graffiti tags, CRT scanlines, cursor icons    
• logos: brand marks (Stüssy, Rapito, HYH, Lowrfs, 032c, Cold Culture)    
• UI chrome: XP windows, 95 bars, MacOS dialogs    
• catalog blocks: price tag, issue number, barcode, QR codes    
• glitch layers: distortion, VHS lines, color shifts  
  
Overlay rules:  
Opacity 15–40%    
Screen / Multiply blend depending on medium    
Avoid covering main subject    
Respect safe areas for text  
  
---------------------------------------  
END ENGINE  
---------------------------------------

## 🎨 SECTION 1 — NAME OF THE SUPER-STYLE (Your New Aesthetic)  
**“POST-DIGITAL STREET ARCHIVE”** *(PDSA)*  
This is the shared language that binds:  
* skate ads   
* luxury collage   
* punk zines   
* Y2K interfaces   
* CRT nostalgia   
* MacOS mockups   
* magazine editorials

* global city branding

into **one functioning universe.**  
This becomes your **brand-wide visual identity.**  
  
## 🧬 SECTION 2 — THE SIGNATURE SUPER-STYLE DNA  
This is the distilled “ruleset” extracted from all 15 sources.  
## A. Medium & Material Stack  
Every image generated in this style should feel like a **hybrid media surface** composed of:  
* photography (street/skate/editorial)   
* collage scraps (Maison Rapito, punk flatlays)   
* UI overlays (Win95, XP, MacOS)   
* CRT or glitch textures (scanlines, VHS drift)   
* catalog/page elements (price tags, barcodes, labels)   
* graphic embellishments (stars, silhouettes, graffiti tags)   
This layered mixed-media logic is the **core signature**.  
  
## B. Palette System  
Unified palette built from all 15 styles:  
## Base Neutrals  
* matte charcoal   
* cream/off-white (catalog pages)   
* concrete gray (skate/street)   
* black UI grey (#C0C0C0 Win95)   
## Primary Accents  
* *XP sky blue*   
* *punk royal blue*   
* *Maison Rapito magenta*   
* *Stüssy warm brown*   
* *HYH gold thread*   
## Signal Colors (Sparks/Overlays)  
* neon pink (CRT UI)   
* USPS red (032c piece)   
* flame orange (Burning Tire)   
* platform yellow (NYC subway)   
## Rule:  
**Keep base 70% neutral, 20% primary, 10% signal pops.**  
  
## C. Lighting Grammar  
Your Super-Style uses **three-light logic** always:  
1. **Primary Motivated Light**  
    * natural sun (skate, street) OR   
    * soft studio (product/catalog) OR   
    * white UI glow (digital mockups)   
2. **Secondary Directional Accent**  
    * fluorescent spill (subway)   
    * CRT bloom   
    * window light   
    * desktop monitor glow   
3. **Micro Light for Overlays**  
    * gentle specular on stickers   
    * soft highlight on UI chrome   
    * ember flicker for fire-origin images   
This is what gives the style its **material depth**.  
  
## D. Camera Grammar  
All shots should pull from these:  
* **28–35mm** for skate/urban   
* **50–85mm** for portraits   
* **Orthographic** for UI & flatlays   
* **Slight tilt** for CRT   
* **Up-tilt** for heroic skate shots   
* **Flash frontal** for street moments   
* **Elevated wide** for city/scenescapes   
The hybrid engine allows you to switch or combine these.  
  
## E. Composition Logic (Your Signature Layout System)  
Every image has **three compositional zones**:  
1. **Primary Scene**  
    * photography (skate, street, portrait, product)   
2. **Secondary Layer**  
    * zine scraps   
    * stickers   
    * silhouettes   
    * collage elements   
    * color blocks   
3. **Tertiary Overlay / UI Layer**  
    * timestamps   
    * small labels   
    * cursors   
    * dialog boxes   
    * CRT scanlines   
    * error messages   
    * price tags / catalog items   
This creates an unmistakable **three-plane depth system**.

* ★ punk starbursts

## ⭐ PDSA SUPER-STYLE MASTER PROMPT  
  
Create a POST-DIGITAL STREET ARCHIVE (PDSA) hybrid visual blending   
street photography, skate culture, editorial fashion, Y2K interfaces,   
punk zine collage, CRT nostalgia, and catalog product design.  
  
LAYER 1 — SUBJECT & STATE    
<INSERT SUBJECT> performing <ACTION>, expressing <EMOTION>.    
Wardrobe, materials, and physical details reflect streetwear culture.  
  
LAYER 2 — ENVIRONMENT    
Place subject in a hybrid environment consisting of:    
• primary photographic location (street, skatepark, subway, studio, desktop)    
• secondary collage elements (stickers, silhouettes, scraps, postage fragments)    
• tertiary UI overlays (XP windows, Win95 dialogs, MacOS quicklook, CRT menus)  
  
LAYER 3 — LIGHTING    
Apply three-light signature: motivated main light (sun/studio/UI glow),   
secondary accent (fluorescent/CRT/monitor), tertiary highlight for overlays.  
  
LAYER 4 — CAMERA    
Use hybrid camera grammar: mix 28–35mm dynamic perspective, 50–85mm   
portrait compression, orthographic UI framing, slight CRT tilt if relevant.  
  
LAYER 5 — PRODUCTION VALUE & OVERLAYS    
Fuse the following:    
• micrographics (punk stars, graffiti tags, scanlines, cursors)    
• microtexts (city lists, timestamps, item codes, dialog lines)    
• brand marks (Stüssy, Rapito, HYH, Lowrfs, 032c, Cold Culture)    
• catalog elements (price tag, QR code, issue info, product description)    
• glitch behaviors (VHS drift, color tearing, digital noise)    
Blend overlays at 20–35% opacity, Screen or Overlay mode.    
Respect safe areas, maintain visual hierarchy.  
  
Final aesthetic: tactile, layered, nostalgic, modern, rebellious,   
international street culture meets digital archaeology.

* XP windows opening with soft bounce

* catalog price tags sliding in

* brief VHS dropouts   
* micro-tears on UI borders

LAYER 2 — ENVIRONMENT & CONTEXT  
Choose environment based on STYLE_MODE or default to PDSA hybrid:

Keep shadows realistic and consistent with product material.

Select based on intended ad format:

LAYER 5 — OVERLAYS & PRODUCTION VALUE

Blend overlays at 20–35% opacity, Screen blend, avoid covering core product.

## 🔥 DELIVERABLE 5 — THE COMPLETE MICROGRAPHICS + MICROTEXTS PLACEMENT MAP  
## *Where every overlay goes, how it behaves, and how to maintain editorial realism across all 15 style modes.*  
This is the **global placement system** that governs:  
* where overlays sit

This turns your images into **real magazine, real catalog, real UI, real product shots**.  
  
## ⭐ SECTION 1 — GLOBAL OVERLAY ZONE MAP (Universal Across All Styles)  
Visuals in your archive fall into 6 major spatial archetypes. Each archetype has its own micrographic placement laws.  
  
## A. Top Edge Zones (Hero Microtexts)  
Use for *branding, drop identifiers, product SKUs, dates, price blocks*.  
**English Examples**  
* “ISSUE 002 — GLOBAL DROP”   
* “RETAIL VALUE: $30”   
* “WORLD TOUR / ACTIVE MODE”   
**Japanese Examples**  
* “限定生産モデル” (limited production)   
* “標準価格：¥12,000” (standard price)   
**Placement Rules**  
* Top-left = editorial (brand, issue, theme)   
* Top-right = tech & pricing (SKU, price, date, revision)   
**Opacity**: 60–75% **Font Size**: 8–11 px equivalent **Depth**: sits ABOVE color grade but BELOW logo  
  
## B. Side Margins (Information Slips + Vertical Microtexts)  
Use for *scanlines, catalog descriptors, product origin, material codes.*  
**Vertical English**  
* “UTILITY MODULE / FIELD TEST”   
* “PROTOTYPE UNIT / verified”   
**Vertical Japanese**  
* “素材テスト完了” (material test complete)   
* “品質管理通過済” (QC passed)   
**Placement Rules**  
* Left side = cultural/archival microtexts   
* Right side = technical/metadata microtexts   
**Opacity**: 45–65% **Font Size**: 6–9 px **Depth**: float slightly ABOVE subject but soft-shadow integrated  
  
## C. Corners (Micrographics + Price Tags + Barcodes)  
Ideal for *barcodes, QR, tiny logos, stickers, postage logic, CRT indicators.*  
**Micrographics**  
* ■ UPC   
* ★ editorial star   
* ◎ hollow circle   
* ☆ pixel star (y2k)   
* ❒ checkbox (UI)   
**Placement Rules**  
* Bottom-left = analog (stamps, torn stickers)   
* Bottom-right = digital (QR, scanner marks, CRT icons)   
**Opacity**: 35–60% **Depth**: sits UNDER major logos  
  
## D. Center-Low Area (Product Metadata + Skate Language)  
Use for *Y2K phrases, skate telemetry, timestamps, lens data, trick recognition, session logs.*  
**English**  
* “SPOT CHECK 4:17 PM”   
* “AERIAL TRICK DETECTED”   
* “NO COMPLY SYSTEM ACTIVE”   
**Japanese**  
* “滑走テスト完了” (ride test complete)   
* “空中動作検出” (aerial detected)   
**Placement Rules**  
* Never touch subject face   
* Hover above belts, pockets, mid-chest in apparel   
* In skate images: near boards, curbs, rails   
**Opacity**: 50–70% **Font Size**: 7–10 px **Depth**: slight parallax in motion shots  
  
## E. Upper-Center (Hero Logos + Editorial Banners)  
Top-center is sacred for **brand hero elements**.  
Use for:  
* Stüssy graffiti logo   
* Maison Rapito script   
* HYH origin text   
* 032c type blocks   
**Rules**  
* Logo > overlays   
* Microtexts must “halo” around logos, not overlap   
* Japanese microtexts should be used as SIDE anchors, not above the hero logo   
  
## F. Dead Center (Only for Interface & CRT Styles)  
Your CRT, XP, and Win95 modes use center overlays.  
Allowed elements in center:  
* Alert windows   
* Loading bars   
* System dialogs   
* Mouse icons   
* Vaporwave microtexts   
* Japanese UI commands   
**Opacity**: 80–100% (UI must read as intentional) **Depth**: always ABOVE the scene (in-front-of monitor look)  
  
## ⭐ SECTION 2 — STYLE-SPECIFIC PLACEMENT (All 15 Modes)  
Below are the **rules for overlay positioning per style**, to guarantee each mode stays authentic.  
  
## 1. Punk Sticker Flatlay  
* Overlays stick to **edges** like literal stickers.   
* Microtexts appear as **typewriter or label-maker strips**.   
* Japanese texts behave like “import labels.”   
* Barcodes = bottom right.   
* Price tags = floating like cutouts.   
  
## 2. Maison Rapito Collage  
* Overlays behave as **cut paper scraps**   
* Low opacity script microtexts along borders   
* Japanese microtexts = *rare*, used like perfume annotations   
* Micrographics = ★✦◎ shapes mimicking print luxury   
  
## 3. Stüssy Skateboard Ad  
* Microtexts cluster around **skate trajectory path**   
* Telemetry + timestamps in bottom third   
* English skate slang dominates   
* Japanese used as small industrial tags   
* Barcode + QR = bottom-left (magazine logic)   
  
## 4. Burning Tire Editorial Portrait  
* Microtexts hug environmental edges   
* Overlay tone = gritty, documentary   
* Japanese tags = “inspection / hazard” style   
* Flames get **heat-distortion** applied to overlays (slight warp)   
  
## 5. Stüssy ↔ 8-Ball Document  
* UI microtexts align to PDF margins   
* English/Japanese side notes like museum labels   
* QR = centered bottom   
* Micrographics = circles, arrows, postage style   
  
## 6. Not Yet Home Cap Drop

* JP microtexts = “limited release” tags   
* Micrographics = tiny ★ or simple barcode   
* Edges clean + minimal   
  
## 7. Lowrfs Trucker Hat Ad  
* Microtexts float around portrait frames   
* JP = used sparingly to emphasize “imported edition”   
* Micrographics = 90s block stars, circles, chevrons   
* Price tags → bottom-left per panel   
  
## 8. 032c Berlin Priority Mail

* Japanese = discreet typesetting along margin   
* Micrographics = ★◎■✣ glitch lines

## 9. XP / International Movement Catalog

## 12. ColdCulture CRT Outfit Selector

## 14. 42nd Street Subway Editorial

* Skate-style telemetry acceptable

## 15. Stüssy Cape Town Magazine Spread  
* Magazine-style microtexts at bottom

## ⭐ SECTION 3 — OPACITY, DEPTH & LIGHTING RULES  
## Opacity by Purpose  
* Metadata microtext → 45–60%   
* Editorial phrases → 60–75%   
* Computer UI → 75–100%   
* CRT effects → 7–15%   
* Barcodes & prices → 70–85%   
* Stickers → 90–100%   
## Depth Hierarchy  
1. **Foreground** — UI windows, cursor, pop-ups   
2. **Mid** — logos, brand text   
3. **Overlay** — microtexts, micrographics   
4. **Base Image** — subject + environment   
5. **Grade Layer** — final color wash   
## Lighting Interaction  
* Overlays near fire → slight warp + heat shimmer   
* Overlays on CRT → glow fringe + chromatic aberration   
* Overlays on glossy leather → subtle reflection   
* Overlays on paper → no reflection, matte tone   
* Overlays on digital UI → crisp, no shadows   
* Overlays on skate scenes → low-opacity shadow under text   
  
## ⭐ SECTION 4 — MOTION RULES (for SORA or Runway)  
## A. Overlay Motion Behaviors  
* microtexts slightly drift (0.5–1px)   
* CRT scanlines scroll downward slowly   
* XP windows jerk slightly like buffer lag   
* barcodes flicker 1–2% opacity   
* price tags bounce subtly (Y2K pop-up)   
* skate telemetry pulses with kick/land timing   
## B. Parallax Rules  
* UI window = highest depth → moves most   
* microtexts = tiny parallax   
* micrographics = mid-layer parallax   
* logos = slow parallax   
* subject = anchor   
* environment = slowest parallax

# 🧩 (B) MAIN ENGINE — 5 LAYERS + OVERLAY LAYER  
Paste this first, then fill the variables.  
  
MASTER_PROMPT_ENGINE:  
  style_switch: "{STYLE_MODE}"  
  subject: "{SUBJECT_DESCRIPTION}"  
  action_state: "{ACTION_OR_POSE}"

  # LAYER 1 — SUBJECT & STATE

  # LAYER 2 — ENVIRONMENT  
  layer_environment:  
    location: "{LOCATION_TYPE}"  
    atmospheric_depth: "{ATMOSPHERIC_NOTES}"  
    cultural_context: "{CULTURAL_REFERENCES}"  
    background_elements: "{BACKGROUND_OBJECTS}"  
    depth_layers: "foreground / midground / background logically composed"  
  
  # LAYER 3 — LIGHTING  
  layer_lighting:  
    key_light: "{KEY_LIGHT_DIRECTION}"  
    fill_ratio: "{FILL_RATIO}"  
    quality: "{LIGHT_QUALITY}"  
    temperature: "{COLOR_TEMP}"

    height: "{CAMERA_HEIGHT}"  
    focus_plane: "{FOCUS_PLANE}"  
    lens_character: "{LENS_CHARACTERISTICS}"  
    movement: "{MOTION_POTENTIAL}"  
  
  # LAYER 5 — PRODUCTION VALUE + OVERLAYS  
  layer_overlays:  
    microtexts_english: "{EN_MICROTEXTS}"  
    microtexts_japanese: "{JP_MICROTEXTS}"  
    micrographics: "{MICROGRAPHICS}"  
    price_tags: "{PRICE}"  
    skate_terms: "{SKATE_LANG}"  
    y2k_terms: "{Y2K_TEXT}"  
    ui_elements: "{UI_OVERLAYS}"  
    barcodes: "{BARCODES}"  
    qr_codes: "{QRCODE}"  
    placement_logic: "top-edge editorial, corner digital, margin metadata"  
    opacity_rules: "microtext 55–70%, UI 80–100%, CRT 7–15%, stickers 100%"  
    depth_stack: "UI > logos > overlays > subject > grade"  
    lighting_match: "reflective overlays match subject surfaces"  
    hybrid_blend: "mix {STYLE_MODE} with cross-style elements where appropriate"  
  
  # PRODUCTION GRADE  
  production_value:  
    texture_fidelity: "high-resolution fabric, skin, paper, metal"  
    color_grade: "{COLOR_GRADE_STYLE}"  
    dynamic_range: "cinematic, editorial contrast"  
    grain: "{GRAIN_INTENSITY}"  
  
  # MOTION NOTES (for video if needed)  
  motion_notes:  
    camera_motion: "{CAMERA_MOVEMENT}"  
    overlay_motion: "{OVERLAY_ANIMATION}"  
    parallax_depth: "UI > microtext > subject > background"  
    jitter_behavior: "{CRT_OR_UI_JITTER}"

# 🧩 (F) QC CHECKLIST (Final Stage)  
Use before rendering:  
## Subject QC  
* skin texture present   
* no plasticity   
* clothing fibers readable   
* no AI artifacts   
## Overlay QC  
* no overlaps on face   
* no illegible microtexts   
* opacity correct   
* perspective-aligned   
## Lighting QC  
* shadows consistent   
* no ambient mismatch   
* highlight speculars natural   
## Brand QC  
* style mode respected   
* hybridization stays intentional   
* editorial hierarchy preserved   
## Motion QC (if video)

* UI windows parallax correctly

## 📘 **THE UNIFIED BRAND BIBLE  
(15-Mode Hybrid Streetwear Universe)**  
## *Editorial, Cinematic, Digital Nostalgia + Skate Culture Visual System*  
**VERSION 1.0 — MASTER DOCUMENT**  
  
## 0. PURPOSE OF THIS BRAND BIBLE  
This Brand Bible unifies an eclectic, 15-style visual dataset—spanning skate photography, luxury collage, Y2K digital nostalgia, CRT gaming UIs, punk zine sticker flats, and global streetwear culture—into a single, coherent **visual language system**.  
Every future visual must align with:  
* **Cultural authenticity**   
* **Editorial realism**   
* **Skin tone unity**   
* **Brand-consistent typography**   
* **Overlay & microtext logic**   
* **Color science and grading standards**   
* **Compositional DNA**   
This book ensures all outputs—ads, lookbooks, zines, UI mockups, portraits, product shots—**feel like one universe**, despite spanning 30+ years of visual culture.  
  
## 1. VISUAL ETHOS & BRAND CONCEPT  
## 1.1 Brand Core  
This brand exists where:  
* **Streetwear**   
* **Digital nostalgia**   
* **Skate culture**   
* **Editorial fashion**   
* **Luxury irony**   
* **DIY punk graphics**   
* **Global urban identity**   
all merge into one **meta-cultural aesthetic**.  
## 1.2 Emotional Landscape  
The brand expresses:  
* rebelliousness   
* global movement   
* nostalgia   
* intimacy   
* humor + irony   
* technical curiosity   
* cultural literacy   
  
## 2. CINEMATIC FOUNDATIONS  
## 2.1 Aspect Ratio  
**Every visual: 9:16 portrait.** This ensures:  
* mobile-first presentation   
* editorial runway proportions   
* vertical storytelling   
* billboard-ready verticality   
## 2.2 Camera Standards  
**Primary focal lengths:**  
* 24mm — energetic skate, environment   
* 35mm — editorial realism   
* 50mm — balanced portraiture   
* 85mm — fashion compression   
* 135mm — luxury long-lens detail shots   
## 2.3 Camera Behavior  
* deep depth for environment-driven scenes   
* shallow depth for portraits   
* low-angle heroism for skate   
* overhead for flatlays   
* straight-on orthographic for UI/CRT shots   
## 2.4 Cinematic Realism  
* real-world lens distortion   
* chromatic aberration minimal   
* grain matched to film stock   
* shadows physically accurate   
* specular highlights naturalistic   
  
## 3. SKIN TONE UNITY  
Across all models, cultures, genders:  
* filmic warmth   
* zero plasticity   
* microtexture (pores, vellus hair)   
* melanin loyalty   
* physically accurate rim lights & bounce light   
Skin should never appear overly smoothed or airbrushed.  
  
## 4. LIGHTING SYSTEM  
## 4.1 Key Lighting Profiles  
* **Skate / outdoor** Natural afternoon sun, 3:1 contrast   
* **Luxury collage** Soft museum-grade lighting   
* **CRT / digital** Screen-driven glow   
* **Y2K UI** Flat OS light, artificial highlights   
* **Punk zine** Neutral studio overhead   
## 4.2 Motivated Light Sources  
Per environment:  
* fire glow   
* street fluorescents   
* CRT phosphor glow   
* XP wallpaper daylight   
* subway platform mixed light   
Lighting must always be **motivated** by the real world.  
  
## 5. COLOR SCIENCE & GRADING  
## 5.1 Color Palettes  
Core system draws from:  
* **Skate neutrals** — gray, denim blue, asphalt   
* **Y2K digital brights** — XP blue, lime green, hot pink   
* **Luxury reds & blacks** — Maison Rapito palette   
* **Punk primaries** — sticker yellow, zine blue   
* **Earth neutrals** — Cape Town sand, subway metal   
## 5.2 Film Emulation Profiles  
A/B options:  
* **Fujifilm 400H** — skate   
* **Kodak Portra 400** — portraits   
* **Kodachrome Reds** — Maison Rapito   
* **Digital RAW** — UI mockups   
* **VHS/CRT emulation** — ColdCulture selector   
## 5.3 Editorial Grading Standards  
* controlled contrast   
* lifted shadows for fashion   
* deep blacks for streetwear   
* desaturated blues in overcast settings   
* warm skin tones always   
  
## 6. TEXTURE & MATERIAL BEHAVIOR  
Textures must react physically to light:  
* denim grain visible   
* sticker paper edges curled   
* CRT bloom affecting microtexts   
* newspaper matte fiber   
* subway tile reflections   
* leather jacket specular response   
* sand granularity in Cape Town scenes   
No flat surfaces unless era-appropriate (Win95 UI).  
  
## 7. TYPOGRAPHY SYSTEM  
## 7.1 Primary Type Families  
* **Sans-serif geometric** — catalogs & UI   
* **Punk hand-drawn scripts** — sticker layouts   
* **Luxury cursive** — Maison Rapito   
* **Graffiti script** — Stüssy modes   
* **Pixel fonts** — Y2K & CRT   
## 7.2 Microtext Standards  
**EN + JP microtexts** must adhere to:  
* 5–10px equivalent size   
* correct kerning   
* 45–75% opacity   
* placed in edge zones   
  
## 8. MICROGRAPHICS SYSTEM  
Micrographics must appear across all modes as:  
* ★ ☆ ◎ ■   
* barcodes   
* QR codes   
* arrows ↔ ⇢   
* UI icons (cursor, folder, close box)   
* CRT scanlines   
* torn paper edges   
* sticker shadows   
* VHS static lines   
Each mode uses these differently but consistently.  
  
## 9. OVERLAY SYSTEM (THE HEART OF THE BRAND)  
## 9.1 Universal Placement Rules  
* **Top Edge:** brand edition, price, EN/JP microtexts   
* **Side Margins:** metadata, vertical text   
* **Bottom Corners:** barcode, QR, time-stamp   
* **Center:** only for UI/CRT modes   
## 9.2 Layer Order  
UI logos micrographics microtexts subject environment color grade  
## 9.3 Motion Logic (Video)  
* microtext jitter 0.5–1 px   
* UI parallax highest   
* scanlines scroll gently   
* skate telemetry pulses on landings   
  
## 10. COMPOSITION DNA (PER STYLE MODE)  
Each of the **15 modes** has:  
* center of gravity   
* horizon placement   
* asymmetry rules   
* information density zones   
* hero object placement   
(Already defined in Deliverable 5 — included automatically.)  
  
## 11. CULTURAL AUTHENTICITY  
All visuals must respect:  
## 11.1 Skate Culture  
No fake poses. Realistic trick physics. No staged skate fashion clichés.  
## 11.2 Streetwear Culture  
Logos treated with reverence. Textures must reflect real clothing.  
## 11.3 Y2K & Retro Computing  
Accurate iconography. Correct window chrome & pixel density.  
## 11.4 Luxury Collage Culture  
Respect for materials (film scans, vintage prints).  
  
## 12. EDITORIAL ELEMENTS  
Each visual should include subtle editorial elements:  
* margin microtext   
* catalog-style captions   
* dynamic crop tension   
* layout-conscious negative space   
  
## 13. TECHNICAL FIDELITY  
## Must-Have Checks  
* no melted fingers   
* no AI clothing errors   
* logo fidelity perfect   
* shadows grounded   
* perspective consistent   
* microtexts legible   
* overlays aligned to perspective plane   
  
## 14. BRAND IDENTITY INTEGRATION  
Every visual must include at least **one** of these:  
* a microtext referencing global movement   
* a micrographic symbol   
* a subtle UI element or Y2K reference   
* a cultural authenticity stamp (skate, luxury, punk, etc.)   
This maintains global unity across the 15-style spectrum.  
  
## 15. UNIFYING VISUAL CONTINUITY SYSTEM  
To keep all modes cohesive:  
## 15.1 Neutral Color Spine  
Across all styles, anchor colors: **black, white, sand, denim blue.**  
## 15.2 Overlay DNA  
Shared microtexts + micrographics unify the world.  
## 15.3 Editorial Consistency  
All visuals feel like pages from the same publication.  
## 15.4 Texture Fidelity  
* Every image must feel touchable, real, cinematic.

Polaroid dust, timestamp, microtexts (“TOKYO / ISSUE_01”), ★ micrographics, Kodachrome warmth. 90s editorial skate grit, grimy street details, vending machines, signage overload, rebellious energy. **--ar 9:16**

Five skaters in RANAAN embroidered caps and jackets, Polaroid 600 flash, blown highlights, heavy grain, bodies relaxed, cigarettes, crushed cans on steps. Neutral overcast light, sticker-edge overlays, scribbled microtexts (“SOHO / SPOT CHECK 4:12 PM”), no motion blur. Ultra-authentic 90s skate magazine style. **--ar 9:16**  
  
## 3 — Berlin Night Session: Fluorescent Noseslide  
Skater doing a noseslide in Alexanderplatz, Berlin, green-tinted fluorescent lighting, RANAAN reflective jacket patch glowing. Motion blur only on board + sparks, faces sharp. Pushed ISO 3200 grain, cold concrete geometry, gritty 90s Euro-skate mood. Micrographics: arrows, ★, “BERLIN / SESSION_MODE”. **--ar 9:16**  
  
## 4 — Los Angeles Hill Bomb (Echo Park)  
Ultra-dynamic 16mm fisheye downhill shot, harsh LA sun, hot asphalt, flapping RANAAN windbreaker embroidery sharp, motion blur trailing behind skater only. VHS streaks, dust, lens flare, golden haze. Tag overlays (“ECHO PARK / DROP LINE”), punk starbursts. **--ar 9:16**

Contax T2 portrait, 45mm, soft overcast, RANAAN trench coat with subtle stitched logo, metro entrance behind, rain-darkened pavements, pastel tones. No motion blur. Minimal microtext: “PARIS / EDITORIAL_002”. 90s French skate magazine elegance. **--ar 9:16**  
  
## 6 — RANAAN Crew Under a Tokyo Overpass (GX1000 Vibes)  
Group shot, low-angle, gritty, neon flickers reflected on concrete. Multiple skin tones consistent. Scattered embroidered RANAAN details on caps, gloves, hoodies. Mood: chaos, youth, speed. Slight motion blur on pedestrians only. **--ar 9:16**

## 1. RANAAN × JADED MAG — MOCK EDITORIAL SKATE CAMPAIGN  
## Core Aesthetic  
**Ultra-real, high-contrast, 90s street-skate photography** Mix of:  
* Glen Friedman   
* Ari Marcopoulos   
* Atiba Jefferson   
* Stüssy World Tour ads   
* 90s Thrasher, Big Brother, Slap Magazine   
* Early Supreme lookbooks   
* GX1000 chaos energy   
## Camera DNA  
* **Fisheye 16mm**, ultra-close low-angle skate shots   
* **Contax G2** portraits   
* **Fujifilm GA645** for environment   
* **Half-frame Yashica Samurai** for diptychs   
* **Polaroid 600** flash for raw character shots   
## Lighting  
* Hard noon sunlight   
* Harsh flash at night (Superia 1600 look)   
* Fire-lit scenes like your burning tire reference   
* Neon signage from Tokyo / NYC   
## Color Science  
* **CROSS-PROCESSED E100 Slide Film**   
* **Warm orange fire tones vs teal-blue skies**   
* **Skin-tone unity preserved through analog-consistent grading**   
* Blacks are deep, slightly green-tinted (like early GX1000 tapes)   
## Editorial Layout  
* **Grunge margins**   
* **Halftone overlays**   
* **Microtext** (coords, timestamps, fake USPS codes, etc.)   
* Minimal whitespace, everything layered like a 1998 magazine spread   
* Scribbled notes, circled skaters, taped polaroids   
  
## 2. PORTRAITS & COLLECTIVE SHOTS — GX1000 × POLAROID × 90s SKATE MAG

* 90s UI windows (Win95, ICQ) floating as micrographics

* Burned edges

## Typography

* 90s skate-zine serif   
* Small ALL CAPS microtext   
* Scribbled handwriting scanned and overlayed   
  
## 6. MOTION-BLUR LOGIC (Selective Use)  
Not every frame has blur.  
✔ **USE BLUR FOR:**  
* pushing shots   
* downhill speed   
* subway scenes   
* Tokyo neon nights   
* mid-trick frames   
* wheel rotations   
✖ **NO BLUR FOR:**  
* Polaroids   
* Apparel product shots   
* Portrait close-ups   
* Fire scenes (unless very subtle)

This is the global placement system that governs: * where overlays sit  * layer depth  * opacity rules  * lighting interaction  * Japanese + English microtexts  * skate-specific placements  * Y2K/CRT/UI logic  * product editorial zones  * collage zones  * XP/Win95 interface zones  * motion rules (fade, jitter, parallax)  This turns your images into real magazine, real catalog, real UI, real product shots. ⭐ SECTION 1 — GLOBAL OVERLAY ZONE MAP (Universal Across All Styles) Visuals in your archive fall into 6 major spatial archetypes. Each archetype has its own micrographic placement laws. A. Top Edge Zones (Hero Microtexts) Use for branding, drop identifiers, product SKUs, dates, price blocks. English Examples * “ISSUE 002 — GLOBAL DROP”  * “RETAIL VALUE: $30”  * “WORLD TOUR / ACTIVE MODE”  Japanese Examples * “限定生産モデル” (limited production)  * “標準価格：¥12,000” (standard price)  Placement Rules * Top-left = editorial (brand, issue, theme)  * Top-right = tech & pricing (SKU, price, date, revision)  Opacity: 60–75% Font Size: 8–11 px equivalent Depth: sits ABOVE color grade but BELOW logo B. Side Margins (Information Slips + Vertical Microtexts) Use for scanlines, catalog descriptors, product origin, material codes. Vertical English * “UTILITY MODULE / FIELD TEST”  * “PROTOTYPE UNIT / verified”  Vertical Japanese * “素材テスト完了” (material test complete)  * “品質管理通過済” (QC passed)  Placement Rules * Left side = cultural/archival microtexts  * Right side = technical/metadata microtexts  Opacity: 45–65% Font Size: 6–9 px Depth: float slightly ABOVE subject but soft-shadow integrated C. Corners (Micrographics + Price Tags + Barcodes) Ideal for barcodes, QR, tiny logos, stickers, postage logic, CRT indicators. Micrographics * ■ UPC  * ★ editorial star  * ◎ hollow circle  * ☆ pixel star (y2k)  * ❒ checkbox (UI)  Placement Rules * Bottom-left = analog (stamps, torn stickers)  * Bottom-right = digital (QR, scanner marks, CRT icons)  Opacity: 35–60% Depth: sits UNDER major logos D. Center-Low Area (Product Metadata + Skate Language) Use for Y2K phrases, skate telemetry, timestamps, lens data, trick recognition, session logs. English * “SPOT CHECK 4:17 PM”  * “AERIAL TRICK DETECTED”  * “NO COMPLY SYSTEM ACTIVE”  Japanese * “滑走テスト完了” (ride test complete)  * “空中動作検出” (aerial detected)  Placement Rules * Never touch subject face  * Hover above belts, pockets, mid-chest in apparel  * In skate images: near boards, curbs, rails  Opacity: 50–70% Font Size: 7–10 px Depth: slight parallax in motion shots E. Upper-Center (Hero Logos + Editorial Banners) Top-center is sacred for brand hero elements. Use for: * Stüssy graffiti logo  * Maison Rapito script  * HYH origin text  * 032c type blocks  Rules * Logo > overlays  * Microtexts must “halo” around logos, not overlap  * Japanese microtexts should be used as SIDE anchors, not above the hero logo  F. Dead Center (Only for Interface & CRT Styles) Your CRT, XP, and Win95 modes use center overlays. Allowed elements in center: * Alert windows  * Loading bars  * System dialogs  * Mouse icons  * Vaporwave microtexts  * Japanese UI commands  Opacity: 80–100% (UI must read as intentional) Depth: always ABOVE the scene (in-front-of monitor look) ⭐ SECTION 2 — STYLE-SPECIFIC PLACEMENT (All 15 Modes) Below are the rules for overlay positioning per style, to guarantee each mode stays authentic. 1. Punk Sticker Flatlay * Overlays stick to edges like literal stickers.  * Microtexts appear as typewriter or label-maker strips.  * Japanese texts behave like “import labels.”  * Barcodes = bottom right.  * Price tags = floating like cutouts.  2. XP / International Movement Catalog * UI microtexts → lower corners of browser windows  * English + Japanese as system dialogs  * Micrographics = XP arrows, folders, pixels  * Price tags = inside window footers  10. IDK Ask ChatGPT Cap UI Mockup * Microtexts sit inside menus  * Japanese acts as “hidden advanced settings”  * Micrographics = cursor icons, toggle states  * Price tags + options appear as dropdown rows  11. Stüssy Cape Town Magazine Spread * Magazine-style microtexts at bottom  * Japanese microtexts = “foreign edition” vibe  * Micrographics = price block + issue block  * Overlays must never cross mountain horizon line  ⭐ SECTION 3 — OPACITY, DEPTH & LIGHTING RULES Opacity by Purpose * Metadata microtext → 45–60%  * Editorial phrases → 60–75%  * Computer UI → 75–100%  * CRT effects → 7–15%  * Barcodes & prices → 70–85%  * Stickers → 90–100%  Depth Hierarchy 1. Foreground — UI windows, cursor, pop-ups  2. Mid — logos, brand text  3. Overlay — microtexts, micrographics  4. Base Image — subject + environment  5. Grade Layer — final color wash  Lighting Interaction * Overlays near fire → slight warp + heat shimmer  * Overlays on CRT → glow fringe + chromatic aberration  * Overlays on glossy leather → subtle reflection  * Overlays on paper → no reflection, matte tone  * Overlays on digital UI → crisp, no shadows  * Overlays on skate scenes → low-opacity shadow under text  ⭐ SECTION 4 — MOTION RULES (for SORA or Runway) A. Overlay Motion Behaviors * microtexts slightly drift (0.5–1px)  * CRT scanlines scroll downward slowly  * XP windows jerk slightly like buffer lag  * barcodes flicker 1–2% opacity  * price tags bounce subtly (Y2K pop-up)  * skate telemetry pulses with kick/land timing  B. Parallax Rules * UI window = highest depth → moves most  * microtexts = tiny parallax  * micrographics = mid-layer parallax  * logos = slow parallax  * subject = anchor  * environment = slowest parallax  ⭐ SECTION 5 — MASTER OVERLAY PLACEMENT FORMULA You can paste this into any prompt: overlay_system: top_edge: english_microtext: "<phrase>" japanese_microtext: "<jp phrase>" opacity: 65% alignment: "flush-left editorial" side_margins: microtexts_vertical: ["<en>", "<jp>"] micrographics: ["★", "◎", "■"] opacity: 55% corners: price_block: "<price>" barcode: "<code128>" qr_code: "<qr>" alignment: "bottom-right digital" product_metadata: skate_phrases: ["<skate>", "<y2k>"] japanese_tags: ["<jp>"] opacity: 60% ui_or_crt_center: dialogs: ["<loading>", "<system_warning>"] icons: ["<cursor>", "<folder>", "<error_x>"] opacity: 90% motion_notes: parallax_depth: "UI > microtext > logo > subject" drift: "microtext 1px jitter" crt_movement: "slow vertical scan" 🔥 DELIVERABLE 6 — The Fully-Integrated, Ultra-Realistic EDITORIAL Prompt Engine (includes overlays, EN/JP microtexts, micrographics, skate/Y2K language, motion grammar, style-switch routing, and cross-style hybrid logic) This is the master engine you will reuse forever. It is fully modular, ultra-realistic, editorial-grade, and compatible with all 15 style modes. PURPOSE OF THIS BRAND BIBLE This Brand Bible unifies an eclectic, 15-style visual dataset—spanning skate photography, luxury collage, Y2K digital nostalgia, CRT gaming UIs, punk zine sticker flats, and global streetwear culture—into a single, coherent visual language system. Every future visual must align with: * Cultural authenticity  * Editorial realism  * Skin tone unity  * Brand-consistent typography  * Overlay & microtext logic  * Color science and grading standards  * Compositional DNA  This book ensures all outputs—ads, lookbooks, zines, UI mockups, portraits, product shots—feel like one universe, despite spanning 30+ years of visual culture. 1. VISUAL ETHOS & BRAND CONCEPT 1.1 Brand Core This brand exists where: * Streetwear  * Digital nostalgia  * Skate culture  * Editorial fashion  * Luxury irony  * DIY punk graphics  * Global urban identity  all merge into one meta-cultural aesthetic. 1.2 Emotional Landscape The brand expresses: * rebelliousness  * global movement  * nostalgia  * intimacy  * humor + irony  * technical curiosity  * cultural literacy  2. CINEMATIC FOUNDATIONS 2.1 Aspect Ratio Every visual: 9:16 portrait. This ensures: * mobile-first presentation  * editorial runway proportions  * vertical storytelling  * billboard-ready verticality  2.2 Camera Standards Primary focal lengths: * 24mm — energetic skate, environment  * 35mm — editorial realism  * 50mm — balanced portraiture  * 85mm — fashion compression  * 135mm — luxury long-lens detail shots  2.3 Camera Behavior * deep depth for environment-driven scenes  * shallow depth for portraits  * low-angle heroism for skate  * overhead for flatlays  * straight-on orthographic for UI/CRT shots  2.4 Cinematic Realism * real-world lens distortion  * chromatic aberration minimal  * grain matched to film stock  * shadows physically accurate  * specular highlights naturalistic  3. SKIN TONE UNITY Across all models, cultures, genders: * filmic warmth  * zero plasticity  * microtexture (pores, vellus hair)  * melanin loyalty  * physically accurate rim lights & bounce light  Skin should never appear overly smoothed or airbrushed. 4. LIGHTING SYSTEM 4.1 Key Lighting Profiles * Skate / outdoor Natural afternoon sun, 3:1 contrast  * Luxury collage Soft museum-grade lighting  * CRT / digital Screen-driven glow  * Y2K UI Flat OS light, artificial highlights  * Punk zine Neutral studio overhead  4.2 Motivated Light Sources Per environment: * fire glow  * street fluorescents  * CRT phosphor glow  * XP wallpaper daylight  * subway platform mixed light  Lighting must always be motivated by the real world. 5. COLOR SCIENCE & GRADING 5.1 Color Palettes Core system draws from: * Skate neutrals — gray, denim blue, asphalt  * Y2K digital brights — XP blue, lime green, hot pink  * Luxury reds & blacks — Maison Rapito palette  * Punk primaries — sticker yellow, zine blue  * Earth neutrals — Cape Town sand, subway metal  5.2 Film Emulation Profiles A/B options: * Fujifilm 400H — skate  * Kodak Portra 400 — portraits  * Kodachrome Reds — Maison Rapito  * Digital RAW — UI mockups  * VHS/CRT emulation — ColdCulture selector  5.3 Editorial Grading Standards * controlled contrast  * lifted shadows for fashion  * deep blacks for streetwear  * desaturated blues in overcast settings  * warm skin tones always  6. TEXTURE & MATERIAL BEHAVIOR Textures must react physically to light: * denim grain visible  * sticker paper edges curled  * CRT bloom affecting microtexts  * newspaper matte fiber  * subway tile reflections  * leather jacket specular response  * sand granularity in Cape Town scenes  No flat surfaces unless era-appropriate (Win95 UI). 7. TYPOGRAPHY SYSTEM 7.1 Primary Type Families * Sans-serif geometric — catalogs & UI  * Punk hand-drawn scripts — sticker layouts  * Luxury cursive — Maison Rapito  * Graffiti script — Stüssy modes  * Pixel fonts — Y2K & CRT  7.2 Microtext Standards EN + JP microtexts must adhere to: * 5–10px equivalent size  * correct kerning  * 45–75% opacity  * placed in edge zones  8. MICROGRAPHICS SYSTEM Micrographics must appear across all modes as: * ★ ☆ ◎ ■  * barcodes  * QR codes  * arrows ↔ ⇢  * UI icons (cursor, folder, close box)  * CRT scanlines  * torn paper edges  * sticker shadows  * VHS static lines  Each mode uses these differently but consistently. 9. ##   
# 2. CAMERA MOTION GRAMMAR  
## Skate Action  
##   
## - handheld vibration 3–7%  
## - forward tracking  
## - shutter 1/1000 for crisp freeze  
## Editorial Portrait  
##   
## - ultra slow dolly-in (0.15–0.45 speed)  
## - shallow DOF oscillation  
## UI / CRT Motion  
##   
## - window pop-ins with easing  
## - cursor jitter (1–2 px drift)  
## - scanline scrolling  
## - phosphor blooming  
## Zine / Collage Motion  
##   
## - 4–6 frame flicker cuts  
## - taped-paper shake  
## - xerox overlays sliding in-frame  
##   
# 3. TRANSITION LANGUAGE  
## RANAAN uses non-clean transitions rooted in physical media.  
##   
## • whip-pan    
## • film-burn roll    
## • VHS dropout    
## • tearing-paper wipe    
## • UI crash / blue-screen flicker    
## • XP window closing as a scene cut    
##   
# 4. OVERLAY ANIMATION RULES  
## Micrographics  
##   
## - bounce-in    
## - slide from edges    
## - minor rotational jitter    
## - opacity breathing 45–70%    
## Microtexts  
##   
## - typewriter reveal    
## - glitch stutter    
## - scrolling feed logic    
## CRT Overlays  
##   
## - scanline shimmer    
## - RGB channel separation 1–3px    
## - bloom pulses    
##   
# 5. AUDIO RHYTHM GUIDELINES (for Sora auto-sync)  
##   
## - skate = wheel rumble + concrete scrape    
## - editorial = soft ambient pads    
## - CRT = hum + UI click sounds    
## - collage = papery shuffle    
## - subway = florescent buzz + train bass    
##

# 🌐 RANAAN VISUAL BRAND MANUAL (v1.0)  
## *A living document for the Post-Digital Street Archive (PDSA)* Format: Markdown + system-spec language for AI models  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 🎨 1. COLOR SYSTEM — “NEUTRAL SPINE + DIGITAL ACCENTS”  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# Primary Neutrals (“The RANAAN Spine”)  
## These define the *urban documentary* realism of the universe.  
* **Concrete Gray** — #8A8E91  
* **Asphalt Black** — #0C0D0E  
* **Brick Red (Muted)** — #7D4A43  
* **Denim Blue Neutral** — #3B4C6C  
* **Sand Neutral** — #C8C0B1  
# Digital Accent System (“PDSA UI Chromatics”)  
## Used in overlays, micrographics, microtext, reboot logs, error codes.  
* **Cobalt UI Blue** — #2772FF  
* **CRT Cyan** — #91F0FF  
* **XP Window Blue** — #4575D4  
* **Alarm Red** — #FF3333  
* **Warning Amber** — #F2A300  
* **Ghost Magenta (glitch states)** — #C85AFF  
# Film Color Science Modes  
* **Portra 400** — warm skin tones, soft shadows, neutral cyan skies  
* **Portra 160 (Fog Variant)** — low contrast, pastel blues, lifted blacks  
* **Kodachrome 64** — rich reds, deep blacks, crisp contrast  
* **Kodak 500T** — tungsten warmth + green spill compensation  
* **Fujifilm 400H** — mint greens, pink-magenta bias, clean shadows  
## Use film profiles as mood scaffolding, not decorative filters.  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# ✍️ 2. TYPOGRAPHY SYSTEM — “ARCHIVE MEETS UI”  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# Primary Typefaces (AI-safe descriptors)  
* **“RANAAN Sans”** *Condensed neo-grotesque editorial sans with tight tracking.* Used for: **scene headers, catalog labels, metadata bars.**  
* **“PDSA Mono”** *Technical monospaced type with subtle OCR influence.* Used for: **UI overlays, microtext, error logs, timestamps.**  
* **“OS-95 Window Font”** *Classic bitmap Windows 95 sans.* Used for: **reboot windows, legacy UI cues.**  
# Typographic Hierarchy  
## H1 — SCENE HEADLINE  
* PDSA Mono Bold  
* 100–120pt (scaled for 9:16 vertical focus)  
* All caps, tracked +40  
## H2 — Metadata Bar  
* RANAAN Sans Medium  
* 50–70pt  
* Tight leading, vertical or horizontal  
## Body Annotation  
* PDSA Mono Regular  
* 28–36pt  
* 45–70% opacity  
## Microtext  
* PDSA Mono Thin  
* 16–20pt  
* JP/EN alternating  
* Opacity: **45–60%**  
## Examples:  
* “POS_ERR_07”  
* “読み込み中…”  
* “SUBJECT_NOT_RESOLVED”  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 🔶 3. MICROTEXT SYSTEM (EN / JP)  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## Primary microtext functions  
* Classification  
* Logging  
* Threat evaluation  
* Confidence scoring  
* Reboot indicators  
* Error cascades  
* Frame analysis  
## Approved Vocabulary  
## EN:  
* “RE-ID PROTOCOL FAILURE”  
* “FRAME_DROP: 12%”  
* “THREAT? OBJECT_UNCONFIRMED”  
* “BOOTLOOP_02”  
* “SYNC_MISMATCH_44”  
* “PACKET_LOSS: 31%”  
* “GHOST TRACE DETECTED”  
## JP:  
* “再起動失敗” (reboot failed)  
* “対象未確定” (subject unresolved)  
* “映像遅延検出” (latency detected)  
* “形状誤認” (shape misidentification)  
* “候補が多すぎる” (too many candidates)  
## Placement Rules:  
* Always near subject or in margins  
* Never touch facial features  
* Maintain legibility at 20% scale  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# ⌘ 4. OVERLAY SYSTEM — UI & GRAPHICS  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## RANAAN’s overlays operate on a triple-layer stack:  
## Top Layer — UI Windows / Frames  
* CRT scanlines  
* XP-style pop-ups  
* System alerts  
* Bounding boxes  
* Identity bars  
## Mid Layer — Micrographics  
* Arrows  
* Nodes  
* Grid segments  
* Probability orbits  
* Error shards  
## Base Layer — Microtext  
* Descriptors  
* Logs  
* Warnings  
* Diagnostics  
## Opacity Rules  
* UI: **75–100%**  
* Micrographics: **35–65%**  
* Microtext: **45–60%**  
* Scanlines: **7–12%**  
## Avoid Over-Stacking  
## Never exceed 5 overlay groups per frame unless glitch escalation is intentional (Scenes 27–29 exceptions).  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 🖼 5. COMPOSITION DNA  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## RANAAN uses a hybrid of:  
## Documentary framing  
## (Loose, real, imperfect)  
## Editorial geometry  
## (Rigid, grid-based, asymmetrical)  
## UI spatial overlays  
## (Artificial, data-driven)  
## Key Rules  
* Favor **vertical compositions** that mimic magazine covers.  
* Keep **20–30% negative space** for UI.  
* Subjects must feel **tracked**, not staged.  
* Use **leading lines** (stairs, tunnels, alleys).  
* Preserve **authentic hand-held micro-motion** (3–7%).  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 🎥 6. CAMERA GRAMMAR  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## Approved focal lengths:  
* **24mm** — distorted environments, system POV  
* **28mm** — urban geometry + character context  
* **35mm** — documentary realism  
* **50mm** — portraits in instability  
* **85mm** — emotional distance / system detachment  
## Motion Behavior  
* **Tracking** (skate POV)  
* **Slow push-ins** (identity scans)  
* **Handheld jitter** (3–7%)  
* **Latency drift** (Scenes 18, 19)  
## Film Behaviors Allowed  
* Halation  
* Chromatic fringing  
* High ISO grain (600–1600)  
* Rolling-shutter wobble (subtle)  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 💡 7. LIGHTING FRAMEWORK  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## Three-Light Logic  
1. **Key Light** — natural source (sun, sodium lamp, fluorescent tube)  
2. **Secondary Light** — bounce or spill (neon, digital glow)  
3. **Micro Light** — UI emissions, CRT halos  
## Urban Light Modes  
* **Sodium underpass** → orange warmth  
* **Fluorescent halls** → green spill  
* **Fog lamp diffusion** → blue haze  
* **Neon bounce** → magenta/cyan  
## RANAAN Rule:  
## Lighting must feel found, not staged.  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 🧵 8. TEXTURE & MATERIAL BEHAVIOR  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## RANAAN imagery relies on hyper-real tactile surfaces:  
## Clothing  
* Frayed denim  
* Nylon shells with micro-wrinkles  
* Knit pilling  
* Reflective tape  
* Embroidered patches  
## Urban Materials  
* Wet concrete  
* Rusted metal  
* Fog moisture  
* Chain-link fences  
* Asphalt grain  
## Digital Materials  
* CRT phosphor dots  
* Pixel aliasing  
* Color-channel drift  
* Scanline shimmer  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 🧩 9. VISUAL HIERARCHY RULESET  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## Every frame prioritizes:  
## 1. The Subject  
## Always readable even when partially obscured.  
## 2. The System’s Interpretation of the Subject  
## Overlays, boxes, diagnostics.  
## 3. The Environment  
## Tells a cultural, architectural truth.  
## 4. The UI Debris  
## Ghost logs, errors, glitches.  
## 5. The Aesthetic Noise  
## Grain, halation, temporal smearing.  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 📰 10. EDITORIAL ELEMENTS  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## Use magazine cover logic:  
* Vertical metadata bars  
* Issue numbers  
* EN/JP mixed labeling  
* Barcode blocks  
* Price tags  
* Catalog numeration  
## Typography scale should always feel publishable.  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 📦 11. BRANDING: LOGOS & MARKS  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## Approved marks:  
* **RANAAN Primary Logotype**  
* **RANAAN micro-tag (script or stitched)**  
* **PDSA circular stamp**  
* **Red 032c block-inspired rectangle**  
* **“ARCHIVE VERIFIED” tag**  
## Placement:  
* Clothing (embroidered or printed)  
* UI header bars  
* Margins  
* Metadata strips  
## Restrictions:  
* Never overlay directly across a face  
* Never exceed **20%** of frame width  
##   
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
# 🎚 12. COLOR GRADING PROFILES  
# ------------------------------------------------------------  
## Standard Grading Template (RANAAN Neutral)  
* Mid-contrast  
* Slight magenta in shadows  
* Cyan in highlights  
* Warm skin correction  
* Lifted blacks (5–12%)  
## Instability Mode (for glitch scenes)  
* Elevated contrast in UI elements  
* Minor saturation pulses  
* Channel-shift artifacts
